Hi, Doctor. Thanks for seeing me today. I, uh... I hope I'm not wasting your time or anything. I just came in because... well, things at my apartment have been a little stressful lately.

Everybody else keeps telling me I'm making a fuss over nothing, that they're just regular notices taped on the doors for everyone, but... well, I just know what's going on.

And I've seen them slipping notes about me to the manager's office. Everybody else keeps telling me I'm making a fuss over nothing, that they're just regular notices taped on the doors for everyone, but... well, I just know what's going on. \n\nI hope it's alright that I'm bringing this up to you. Well, um... I guess sometimes people do get a little confused when I'm talking. But I don't think it's because I'm disorganized! I just have a lot on my mind, you know? 

Like, if I try to explain things to my sister, I might start talking about the rent being late, and then... well, then I'll remember the boats down at the marina, and how they relate to the choir robes at the church, and... and the wires they put in the walls here. And she just looks at me like I skipped half the story. 

I know it probably sounds like a lot jumping around, but it all connects in my head. I just... I might not always explain the connections perfectly out loud. I try really hard to be clear, though. I wouldn't want anyone to think I'm not making sense. Does that make sense to you? I hope I'm explaining it okay.

No, no, I've never had anything like that. I actually... well, I sleep quite a bit, to tell you the truth. Sometimes I have a hard time even getting out of bed in the morning.

I hope that doesn't complicate things for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being put into your mind or controlled by someone else?\n\nPatient: Oh, no. No, my thoughts are definitely my own. I wouldn't want you to think I'm losing control of my own mind or anything like that. I know I've been a little... well, focused on the neighbors, but I'm still the one thinking my own thoughts. \n\nI've never felt like someone was putting things in my head or making me think a certain way. I really try to stay grounded, you know? I hope that's the right answer. I just want to reassure you that I'm still very much myself in that way. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard or seen anything that seemed like a clear message specifically meant for you?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I don't know if I'd call it a message, exactly. I mean, I know I mentioned the notices on the doors before. The ones people keep telling me are just regular apartment updates? \n\nI... well, I know they're actually slipping notes about me to the office. It's just... it's the way they do it when I'm coming and going, you know? It feels very specific to me. But I really don't want to sound like I'm making a big deal out of nothing. I know it probably sounds a bit silly when I say it out loud. \n\nI just... I notice these things, and I try to be observant. Is that the kind of thing you meant? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard a voice talking to you when no one else was around?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I've never heard any voices or anything like that. Just my own voice in my head, you know, when I'm thinking to myself. \n\nI... I know sometimes people struggle with that kind of thing, but I promise I don't. I wouldn't want you to worry that I'm, well, completely losing my grip on things. I really do try my best to stay grounded in reality, even with the stress from the neighbors. \n\nI hope that's a relief to hear? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job answering your questions and giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have other people told you that your speech or behavior has become disorganized or hard to follow?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess sometimes people do get a little confused when I'm talking. But I don't think it's because I'm disorganized! I just have a lot on my mind, you know? \n\nLike, if I try to explain things to my sister, I might start talking about the rent being late, and then... well, then I'll remember the boats down at the marina, and how they relate to the choir robes at the church, and... and the wires they put in the walls here. And she just looks at me like I skipped half the story. \n\nI know it probably sounds like a lot jumping around, but it all connects in my head. I just... I might not always explain the connections perfectly out loud. I try really hard to be clear, though. I wouldn't want anyone to think I'm not making sense. Does that make sense to you? I hope I'm explaining it okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period when your mood was unusually elevated or irritable for several days?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I can get a little grumpy sometimes, just like anybody else, especially when I haven't been sleeping well or... or when I'm worrying about the apartment. But never anything unusual or, you know, out of control. \n\nI'm usually a pretty even-keeled guy. I don't really get those big highs or anything like that. I mostly just feel... well, a bit down a lot of the time, to be honest. But definitely not overly excited or anything that would cause a problem. \n\nI try to keep my temper in check, too. I don't like to cause a fuss or bother people. I hope that's the right answer? I just want to make sure I'm not giving you the wrong impression about my mood.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least several days when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: No, no, I've never had anything like that. I actually... well, I sleep quite a bit, to tell you the truth. Sometimes I have a hard time even getting out of bed in the morning. \n\nI've never had a time where I didn't need sleep and felt all energized. If I don't sleep, I just feel terrible and worn out. Well, I... I suppose I have been keeping to myself a bit more lately. But I think that's normal when you're getting older and living alone, right? 

I mean, I used to be quite active. I always looked forward to choir practice, and I played bridge with some folks... I even used to love cooking up a big pot of beans on Sundays, and I'd do little repair jobs around the neighborhood for people who needed a hand. 

But lately... well, I guess I just haven't had the same enthusiasm for it all. Sometimes I just let the phone ring, to be honest. I tell myself there's really no point in going out or getting involved. I hope that doesn't sound too awful? I don't want you to think I'm antisocial or anything. I just... I haven't been feeling up to it as much. Is it okay that I've been taking a step back from those things? I just don't want to bring anyone else down if I'm not feeling my best.

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I don't think I've had anything too unusual besides the... the neighbor situation. I mean, I try to keep my thoughts straight, but I guess sometimes my words get a little jumbled up. 

Like, I'll be trying to explain something simple, and... and I'll start talking about the rent at the apartment, and then somehow I'm talking about the marina down the road, and then the choir robes, and... and then the wires I thought were in the walls. And I look up, and people are just staring at me like I skipped half the story. 

I know it probably sounds a bit confusing. I try really hard to make sense, I promise I do. It's just that my mind kind of jumps from one thing to the next, and I don't realize I've lost people until they give me that look. I hope you don't think I'm losing my mind or anything? I really am trying to communicate clearly. It's just... sometimes it all gets a bit tangled up in my head. Is that... is that something that happens to a lot of people?

I know some people think it's lazy, but I just don't have the energy for much else most days. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, no, sir... I don't think there's been a week where it stopped completely. I know I keep saying this, and I really hope I'm not sounding like a broken record or being difficult... but those thoughts about the neighbors and the folks down at the office, they've been with me for about a year and a month now. 

I try my absolute best to push them away and just be a good, polite neighbor, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just letting my mind run wild. But no, they haven't completely gone away, even for a week. I'm sorry if that's not what you were hoping to hear. Is... is it okay that I'm explaining it this way? I just want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to know.

I hope that doesn't complicate things for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being put into your mind or controlled by someone else?\n\nPatient: Oh, no. I know it probably sounds a bit silly when I say it out loud. \n\nI just... I notice these things, and I try to be observant. Is that the kind of thing you meant? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard a voice talking to you when no one else was around?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I've never heard any voices or anything like that. Just my own voice in my head, you know, when I'm thinking to myself. \n\nI... I know sometimes people struggle with that kind of thing, but I promise I don't. I wouldn't want you to worry that I'm, well, completely losing my grip on things. I really do try my best to stay grounded in reality, even with the stress from the neighbors. \n\nI hope that's a relief to hear? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job answering your questions and giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have other people told you that your speech or behavior has become disorganized or hard to follow?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess sometimes people do get a little confused when I'm talking. But I don't think it's because I'm disorganized! I just have a lot on my mind, you know? \n\nLike, if I try to explain things to my sister, I might start talking about the rent being late, and then... well, then I'll remember the boats down at the marina, and how they relate to the choir robes at the church, and... and the wires they put in the walls here. And she just looks at me like I skipped half the story. \n\nI know it probably sounds like a lot jumping around, but it all connects in my head. I just... I might not always explain the connections perfectly out loud. I try really hard to be clear, though. I wouldn't want anyone to think I'm not making sense. Does that make sense to you? I hope I'm explaining it okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period when your mood was unusually elevated or irritable for several days?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I can get a little grumpy sometimes, just like anybody else, especially when I haven't been sleeping well or... or when I'm worrying about the apartment. But never anything unusual or, you know, out of control. \n\nI'm usually a pretty even-keeled guy. I don't really get those big highs or anything like that. I mostly just feel... well, a bit down a lot of the time, to be honest. But definitely not overly excited or anything that would cause a problem. \n\nI try to keep my temper in check, too. I don't like to cause a fuss or bother people. I hope that's the right answer? I just want to make sure I'm not giving you the wrong impression about my mood.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least several days when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: No, no, I've never had anything like that. I actually... well, I sleep quite a bit, to tell you the truth. Sometimes I have a hard time even getting out of bed in the morning. \n\nI've never had a time where I didn't need sleep and felt all energized. If I don't sleep, I just feel terrible and worn out. I definitely don't get those bursts of energy you're talking about. I'm usually just trying to get through the day without feeling too exhausted. \n\nIs it... is it okay that I sleep so much? I know some people think it's lazy, but I just don't have the energy for much else most days. I hope you don't think poorly of me for that.\n\nDoctor: Has this been going on continuously for six months or longer?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, it's been going on for a while now. I'd say... maybe about a year and five months? \n\nIt started around the time I left the house I shared with my wife and... and moved into the senior apartment. It's just been a lot of adjusting, you know? Being on my own and all. \n\nI know that sounds like a long time to be feeling this way, and I really have been trying to pull myself together. I don't want to be a burden or seem like I'm not trying hard enough to get better. I just... it's been a tough transition, and I guess I haven't quite bounced back like I should have. I hope you understand? I really am trying my best to manage it all.\n\nDoctor: Have you become more socially withdrawn or less interested in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose I have been keeping to myself a bit more lately. But I think that's normal when you're getting older and living alone, right? \n\nI mean, I used to be quite active. I always looked forward to choir practice, and I played bridge with some folks... I even used to love cooking up a big pot of beans on Sundays, and I'd do little repair jobs around the neighborhood for people who needed a hand. \n\nBut lately... well, I guess I just haven't had the same enthusiasm for it all. Sometimes I just let the phone ring, to be honest. I tell myself there's really no point in going out or getting involved. I hope that doesn't sound too awful? I don't want you to think I'm antisocial or anything. I just... I haven't been feeling up to it as much. Is it okay that I've been taking a step back from those things? I just don't want to bring anyone else down if I'm not feeling my best.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had hallucinations, unusual beliefs, or other psychotic symptoms besides the neighbors watching you and slipping notes?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I don't think I've had anything too unusual besides the... the neighbor situation. I mean, I try to keep my thoughts straight, but I guess sometimes my words get a little jumbled up. \n\nLike, I'll be trying to explain something simple, and... and I'll start talking about the rent at the apartment, and then somehow I'm talking about the marina down the road, and then the choir robes, and... and then the wires I thought were in the walls.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m Dr. —. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hi, Doctor. Thanks for seeing me today. I, uh... I hope I'm not wasting your time or anything. I just came in because... well, things at my apartment have been a little stressful lately. \n\nI don't want to sound like I'm just complaining, but I know the folks in my building are watching my comings and goings. And I've seen them slipping notes about me to the manager's office. Everybody else keeps telling me I'm making a fuss over nothing, that they're just regular notices taped on the doors for everyone, but... well, I just know what's going on. \n\nI hope it's alright that I'm bringing this up to you. Does that sound like an okay reason to come in?\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices that other people around you cannot hear?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. I mean, I've got my head on straight, you know? I'm not hearing things that aren't there. I just... I just notice the things people are doing around the building. That's all. I really hope I'm not coming across as crazy or anything. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. Is that what you needed to know?\n\nDoctor: If someone showed you clear proof that the neighbors were not watching you, would you be able to accept that?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I'd certainly try to. I really want to be agreeable, you know? I don't want you to think I'm just being stubborn or difficult. If someone had real proof, I'd definitely look at it and try my best to accept it. \n\nIt's just... it's really hard to ignore what's right in front of me. Even when people keep telling me they're just regular notices taped on the doors, I... well, I still know they're actually slipping notes about me to the office. And I can feel them watching when I come and go. \n\nBut I'd try to listen if you showed me something different. I really do want to be cooperative and do the right thing here. Does that sound like a fair answer? I hope I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you usually been this suspicious and mistrustful of other people for as long as you can remember?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not for my whole life. I've always tried to get along with everyone, honestly. I was never the type to cause trouble or think poorly of people. \n\nIt's really just been... well, maybe for about a year and a month now? That's when I started noticing the things at the apartment. Before that, I was perfectly fine with my neighbors. I don't want you to think I'm just a suspicious person by nature, because I'm really not. I like to see the good in people. \n\nIs it okay that this is a more recent thing? I hope that doesn't complicate things for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being put into your mind or controlled by someone else?\n\nPatient: Oh, no. No, my thoughts are definitely my own. I wouldn't want you to think I'm losing control of my own mind or anything like that. I know I've been a little... well, focused on the neighbors, but I'm still the one thinking my own thoughts. \n\nI've never felt like someone was putting things in my head or making me think a certain way. I really try to stay grounded, you know? I hope that's the right answer. I just want to reassure you that I'm still very much myself in that way. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard or seen anything that seemed like a clear message specifically meant for you?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I don't know if I'd call it a message, exactly. I mean, I know I mentioned the notices on the doors before. The ones people keep telling me are just regular apartment updates? \n\nI... well, I know they're actually slipping notes about me to the office. It's just... it's the way they do it when I'm coming and going, you know? It feels very specific to me. But I really don't want to sound like I'm making a big deal out of nothing. I know it probably sounds a bit silly when I say it out loud. \n\nI just... I notice these things, and I try to be observant. Is that the kind of thing you meant? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard a voice talking to you when no one else was around?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I've never heard any voices or anything like that. Just my own voice in my head, you know, when I'm thinking to myself. \n\nI... I know sometimes people struggle with that kind of thing, but I promise I don't. I wouldn't want you to worry that I'm, well, completely losing my grip on things. I really do try my best to stay grounded in reality, even with the stress from the neighbors. \n\nI hope that's a relief to hear? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job answering your questions and giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have other people told you that your speech or behavior has become disorganized or hard to follow?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess sometimes people do get a little confused when I'm talking. But I don't think it's because I'm disorganized! I just have a lot on my mind, you know? \n\nLike, if I try to explain things to my sister, I might start talking about the rent being late, and then... well, then I'll remember the boats down at the marina, and how they relate to the choir robes at the church, and... and the wires they put in the walls here. And she just looks at me like I skipped half the story. \n\nI know it probably sounds like a lot jumping around, but it all connects in my head. I just... I might not always explain the connections perfectly out loud. I try really hard to be clear, though. I wouldn't want anyone to think I'm not making sense. Does that make sense to you? I hope I'm explaining it okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period when your mood was unusually elevated or irritable for several days?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I can get a little grumpy sometimes, just like anybody else, especially when I haven't been sleeping well or... or when I'm worrying about the apartment. But never anything unusual or, you know, out of control. \n\nI'm usually a pretty even-keeled guy. I don't really get those big highs or anything like that. I mostly just feel... well, a bit down a lot of the time, to be honest. But definitely not overly excited or anything that would cause a problem. \n\nI try to keep my temper in check, too. I don't like to cause a fuss or bother people. I hope that's the right answer? I just want to make sure I'm not giving you the wrong impression about my mood.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least several days when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: No, no, I've never had anything like that. I actually... well, I sleep quite a bit, to tell you the truth. Sometimes I have a hard time even getting out of bed in the morning. \n\nI've never had a time where I didn't need sleep and felt all energized. If I don't sleep, I just feel terrible and worn out. I definitely don't get those bursts of energy you're talking about. I'm usually just trying to get through the day without feeling too exhausted. \n\nIs it... is it okay that I sleep so much? I know some people think it's lazy, but I just don't have the energy for much else most days. I hope you don't think poorly of me for that.\n\nDoctor: Has this been going on continuously for six months or longer?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, it's been going on for a while now. I'd say... maybe about a year and five months? \n\nIt started around the time I left the house I shared with my wife and... and moved into the senior apartment. It's just been a lot of adjusting, you know? Being on my own and all. \n\nI know that sounds like a long time to be feeling this way, and I really have been trying to pull myself together. I don't want to be a burden or seem like I'm not trying hard enough to get better. I just... it's been a tough transition, and I guess I haven't quite bounced back like I should have. I hope you understand? I really am trying my best to manage it all.\n\nDoctor: Have you become more socially withdrawn or less interested in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose I have been keeping to myself a bit more lately. But I think that's normal when you're getting older and living alone, right? \n\nI mean, I used to be quite active. I always looked forward to choir practice, and I played bridge with some folks... I even used to love cooking up a big pot of beans on Sundays, and I'd do little repair jobs around the neighborhood for people who needed a hand. \n\nBut lately... well, I guess I just haven't had the same enthusiasm for it all. Sometimes I just let the phone ring, to be honest. I tell myself there's really no point in going out or getting involved. I hope that doesn't sound too awful? I don't want you to think I'm antisocial or anything. I just... I haven't been feeling up to it as much. Is it okay that I've been taking a step back from those things? I just don't want to bring anyone else down if I'm not feeling my best.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had hallucinations, unusual beliefs, or other psychotic symptoms besides the neighbors watching you and slipping notes?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I don't think I've had anything too unusual besides the... the neighbor situation. I mean, I try to keep my thoughts straight, but I guess sometimes my words get a little jumbled up. \n\nLike, I'll be trying to explain something simple, and... and I'll start talking about the rent at the apartment, and then somehow I'm talking about the marina down the road, and then the choir robes, and... and then the wires I thought were in the walls. And I look up, and people are just staring at me like I skipped half the story. \n\nI know it probably sounds a bit confusing. I try really hard to make sense, I promise I do. It's just that my mind kind of jumps from one thing to the next, and I don't realize I've lost people until they give me that look. I hope you don't think I'm losing my mind or anything? I really am trying to communicate clearly. It's just... sometimes it all gets a bit tangled up in my head. Is that... is that something that happens to a lot of people?\n\nDoctor: Have you had these experiences without any break for at least six months?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, I guess it has been going on for at least that long. I really try not to let it get to me, and I always try to be a good, quiet tenant, you know? I don't want to cause any trouble. \n\nBut... those suspicious thoughts I mentioned, about the neighbors and the people down at the apartment office... they've been with me for about a year and a month now. Pretty much without a break. I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline so I can be helpful. Does that answer what you were asking? I hope I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any times when these suspicious thoughts or confusing speech symptoms lasted less than a month and then went away completely?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, they haven't really gone away at all. Like I think I might have just mentioned—and I'm sorry if I'm repeating myself, I really don't want to waste your time—but those thoughts about the apartment neighbors and the office, they've been with me for about a year and a month now. Pretty much the whole time. \n\nI really wish I could tell you they stopped and went away, because I try so hard to just be normal and not be a bother to anyone. I try to just ignore it all. But they've just kind of stuck around. I hope it's okay that I'm telling you this? I know it probably doesn't sound great, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the honest truth so I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: During this whole time, have you ever had a period of at least a week when the suspiciousness and the odd speech were completely gone?\n\nPatient: Um, no, sir... I don't think there's been a week where it stopped completely. I know I keep saying this, and I really hope I'm not sounding like a broken record or being difficult... but those thoughts about the neighbors and the folks down at the office, they've been with me for about a year and a month now. \n\nI try my absolute best to push them away and just be a good, polite neighbor, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just letting my mind run wild. But no, they haven't completely gone away, even for a week. I'm sorry if that's not what you were hoping to hear. Is... is it okay that I'm explaining it this way? I just want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to know.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt very depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Well... um, yes, I suppose I have. I mean, I really try to keep my chin up, you know? I don't want to be one of those people who just complains all the time. But... yeah, for a while now, most mornings I wake up with this... this heavy, hollow feeling. It's almost like the day already went bad before I even get my feet on the floor. \n\nI try to just push through it and put on a brave face, I really do. I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself. It's just... it's been pretty constant for more than two weeks, yeah. Is it alright that I'm admitting that? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when the suspicious thoughts were present even when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, there was. I hope it's okay to say that. I don't want to sound like I'm contradicting myself or making things confusing. But... there was a spell, maybe for several weeks, where my mood actually lifted a little. I was eating a bit better, and I wasn't waking up with that awful hopeless feeling I told you about. I was really trying to turn over a new leaf, you know?\n\nBut... well, even during that better time, I still... I still couldn't shake the feeling about the neighbors. I still believed they were watching my door and reporting on me. I tried so hard to just ignore it and be a good neighbor, but the thoughts were still there. I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds strange, having those thoughts even when I wasn't feeling so down. I hope I'm explaining this right for you? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the full picture, even if it's not very flattering.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I really try to keep a steady routine, you know? I don't want to cause any trouble by being up all hours of the night. It's just been a lot of adjusting, you know? Being on my own and all. \n\nI know that sounds like a long time to be feeling this way, and I really have been trying to pull myself together. I don't want to be a burden or seem like I'm not trying hard enough to get better. I just... it's been a tough transition, and I guess I haven't quite bounced back like I should have. I hope you understand? I really am trying my best to manage it all.\n\nDoctor: Have you become more socially withdrawn or less interested in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose I have been keeping to myself a bit more lately. But I think that's normal when you're getting older and living alone, right? \n\nI mean, I used to be quite active. I always looked forward to choir practice, and I played bridge with some folks... I even used to love cooking up a big pot of beans on Sundays, and I'd do little repair jobs around the neighborhood for people who needed a hand. \n\nBut lately... well, I guess I just haven't had the same enthusiasm for it all. Sometimes I just let the phone ring, to be honest. I tell myself there's really no point in going out or getting involved. I hope that doesn't sound too awful? I don't want you to think I'm antisocial or anything. I just... I haven't been feeling up to it as much. Is it okay that I've been taking a step back from those things? I just don't want to bring anyone else down if I'm not feeling my best.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had hallucinations, unusual beliefs, or other psychotic symptoms besides the neighbors watching you and slipping notes?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I don't think I've had anything too unusual besides the... the neighbor situation. I mean, I try to keep my thoughts straight, but I guess sometimes my words get a little jumbled up. \n\nLike, I'll be trying to explain something simple, and... and I'll start talking about the rent at the apartment, and then somehow I'm talking about the marina down the road, and then the choir robes, and... and then the wires I thought were in the walls. And I look up, and people are just staring at me like I skipped half the story. \n\nI know it probably sounds a bit confusing. I try really hard to make sense, I promise I do. It's just that my mind kind of jumps from one thing to the next, and I don't realize I've lost people until they give me that look. I hope you don't think I'm losing my mind or anything? I really am trying to communicate clearly. It's just... sometimes it all gets a bit tangled up in my head. Is that... is that something that happens to a lot of people?\n\nDoctor: Have you had these experiences without any break for at least six months?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, I guess it has been going on for at least that long. I really try not to let it get to me, and I always try to be a good, quiet tenant, you know? I don't want to cause any trouble. \n\nBut... those suspicious thoughts I mentioned, about the neighbors and the people down at the apartment office... they've been with me for about a year and a month now. Pretty much without a break. I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline so I can be helpful. Does that answer what you were asking? I hope I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any times when these suspicious thoughts or confusing speech symptoms lasted less than a month and then went away completely?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, they haven't really gone away at all. Like I think I might have just mentioned—and I'm sorry if I'm repeating myself, I really don't want to waste your time—but those thoughts about the apartment neighbors and the office, they've been with me for about a year and a month now. Pretty much the whole time. \n\nI really wish I could tell you they stopped and went away, because I try so hard to just be normal and not be a bother to anyone. I try to just ignore it all. But they've just kind of stuck around. I hope it's okay that I'm telling you this? I know it probably doesn't sound great, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the honest truth so I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: During this whole time, have you ever had a period of at least a week when the suspiciousness and the odd speech were completely gone?\n\nPatient: Um, no, sir... I don't think there's been a week where it stopped completely. I know I keep saying this, and I really hope I'm not sounding like a broken record or being difficult... but those thoughts about the neighbors and the folks down at the office, they've been with me for about a year and a month now. \n\nI try my absolute best to push them away and just be a good, polite neighbor, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just letting my mind run wild. But no, they haven't completely gone away, even for a week. I'm sorry if that's not what you were hoping to hear. Is... is it okay that I'm explaining it this way? I just want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to know.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt very depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Well... um, yes, I suppose I have. I mean, I really try to keep my chin up, you know? I don't want to be one of those people who just complains all the time. But... yeah, for a while now, most mornings I wake up with this... this heavy, hollow feeling. It's almost like the day already went bad before I even get my feet on the floor. \n\nI try to just push through it and put on a brave face, I really do. I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself. It's just... it's been pretty constant for more than two weeks, yeah. Is it alright that I'm admitting that? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when the suspicious thoughts were present even when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, there was. I hope it's okay to say that. I don't want to sound like I'm contradicting myself or making things confusing. But... there was a spell, maybe for several weeks, where my mood actually lifted a little. I was eating a bit better, and I wasn't waking up with that awful hopeless feeling I told you about. I was really trying to turn over a new leaf, you know?\n\nBut... well, even during that better time, I still... I still couldn't shake the feeling about the neighbors. I still believed they were watching my door and reporting on me. I tried so hard to just ignore it and be a good neighbor, but the thoughts were still there. I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds strange, having those thoughts even when I wasn't feeling so down. I hope I'm explaining this right for you? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the full picture, even if it's not very flattering.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you had these suspicious beliefs even though you were not feeling depressed at all?\n\nPatient: Yes, sir. I was eating a bit better and not waking up so hopeless like I usually do. But... well, even during those good weeks, I still believed the neighbors were watching my door and reporting on me. I really tried to shake it off, I did. \n\nI hope it's okay that I'm repeating that for you. Did I explain that right this time? I just want to be absolutely sure I'm giving you the exact details you're looking for.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least one week when you felt unusually energized or needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I just want to make sure I'm doing things the right way. Is that the kind of answer you needed?"
